  • Outcome: the Euclidean Hilbert-space/semigroup argument is valid conditional on its explicitly displayed correlation hypotheses. Several small statement corrections are requested for exact public correspondence. The existence of correlations satisfying those hypotheses and the passage to the relativistic endpoint remain outstanding; they are not established by this local argument.

Required statement corrections

  1. At ex-statement, initial lines 24–25, specify that both integer sequences are strictly increasing. YangMills.WilsonFamily.periodStrict and .denominatorStrict in Lean/PublicWilson.lean assert StrictMono. Mere nondecrease would not imply diverging volume and vanishing mesh.

  2. At initial lines 55–58, say that the grade-space maps into the common label space are complex-linear injections. Hierarchy.toLabel is a complex-linear map; Reconstructed.injective supplies injectivity. “Map injectively” omits linearity.

  3. At ex-reconstructed, initial lines 77–80, state that the field is complex-linear separately in the label, the test, and the domain vector. FieldSystem.field in Lean/PublicTheory.lean is three nested linear maps. The current phrase “complex-linear fields Phi(A,f):D->D” directly states only linearity in the domain vector.

  4. At initial line 93, add that at least one label and test give a nonzero field operator. Faithfulness says every nonzero label is seen; it alone permits a zero label space. The public reconstruction explicitly includes the separate Hierarchy.Reconstructed.acts clause in Lean/ChallengeSpec.lean. A later trace conclusion might imply nontriviality with additional reasoning, but the definition should preserve the explicit endpoint condition rather than rely on an unproved implication.

The endpoint's order of choices, separation of selected and mixed correlations, full-mesh fixed-row limits, subsequent volume refinement, and restricted time-analytic boundary interface are otherwise faithfully represented in the reviewed statement. Its deferred OPE and trace definitions were not present in the initial two-file review and require separate checking when supplied.

Euclidean Hilbert-space argument

The following checks found no substantive gap in the conditional proof.

Manuscript step Mathematical check Source correspondence
Reflection and positive kernel Positive words have distinct points internally; reflected and unreflected parts lie in opposite strict half-spaces, so cross coincidences cannot occur. Reflection reverses concatenation and squares to the identity. Applying the reflection identity to (theta v)w yields Hermitian symmetry. The displayed positivity assumption gives a positive semidefinite kernel on all positive words, including the empty word. YM.OS.reflect_twice, twice_expect, nonnegative in Lean/YM/OS/Schwinger.lean; YM.Table.Hierarchy.positive in Lean/YM/Euclid/Words.lean
Separated bound implies cap Within-word sup distances are fixed. A cross-pair time difference is the sum of two positive original times and the nonnegative translation time. A single positive rho works for all finitely many pairs and all half-space translations. Empty and one-point configurations are correctly covered by the no-pairs case. Smoothness composed with the continuous configuration map gives relative continuity on the closed half-space. YM.OS.uniformAway, correl in Lean/YM/OS/Caps.lean; derivative-order-zero case of YM.Table.Capped in Lean/YM/Euclid/Table.lean
Cauchy–Schwarz and null quotient The finite sums define a Hermitian positive semidefinite sesquilinear form, conjugate-linear in its first variable. Minimizing the quadratic in the nonzero-diagonal case, and scaling a complex parameter in the zero-diagonal case, prove Cauchy–Schwarz. Thus a null vector pairs to zero with every vector; sums and scalar multiples of null vectors are null. The induced quotient form is well-defined and positive definite. Completion gives the asserted dense word span. YM.Kernel.core, host in Lean/YM/Hilbert/Kolmogorov.lean; the source uses separated completion of a seminormed space rather than spelling out the intermediate quotient. These are equivalent constructions.
Uniform time bound Cap boundedness for the repeated word controls its translated norm squared. For finitely many words, the triangle inequality supplies a bound depending on that finite linear combination. No bound uniform over all words, labels or arities is claimed or needed. YM.OS.enough in Lean/YM/OS/Flow.lean
Contraction before descent Defining translated vectors on formal sums avoids assuming null-space invariance. The translation identity yields norm(T_s c)^2 = <T_0 c,T_{2s} c>. Cauchy–Schwarz bounds this by a times the finite supremum M. Since the norms are nonnegative and bounded, the supremum of their squares is M squared. Thus M squared <= aM, hence M <= a, including M=0. This proves descent without circularity. YM.OS.symmetrical, contract in Lean/YM/OS/Flow.lean; YM.Kernel.Gram.by_bound in Lean/YM/Hilbert/Kolmogorov.lean
Semigroup and symmetry Time translations preserve positive words; their composition law descends and extends by density. Setting one time parameter to zero gives symmetry on the dense span; boundedness extends it to the whole Hilbert space. An everywhere-defined bounded symmetric operator is self-adjoint. YM.OS.sym_time, advances; YM.OS.implement in Lean/YM/OS/Dynamics.lean
Spatial unitary group Spatial shifts preserve positive words and commute with reflection. Translation invariance preserves all Gram pairings, so formal shifts preserve norms and descend. The inverse spatial shift proves surjectivity and unitarity. Commutation with time translations is verified on words and extends by density. YM.OS.sym_space, parallel, switch; YM.OS.safe, implement
Strong continuity For time translations the squared difference norm is expressed through the cap-continuous correlation at 2s, 2t and s+t. For spatial translations, diagonal terms are constant and the cross term is cap-continuous with the first shifted word held fixed; all spatial differences have zero time component. Finite sums are continuous. Approximation by a finite sum and the uniform operator bound 1 gives continuity for every vector. No operator-norm continuity is assumed. YM.OS.ketC, contE, contU, implement in Lean/YM/OS/Dynamics.lean; YM.OS.strong in Lean/YM/OS/Flow.lean

Two notation corrections would make the proof cleaner without changing it:

  • At ex-time-symmetry, initial line 95, replace “Bilinearity” with “Sesquilinearity.”

  • In ex-contraction, initial line 110, write the absolute value of the complex inner product before applying Cauchy–Schwarz. Its equality to the nonnegative norm square already ensures the current expression has a real value; the modulus makes that fact explicit and avoids ordering a visibly complex expression.

The cap lemma is an accurate consequence of the source's stronger derivative estimates. It does not establish those estimates. In particular Table.Capped permits constants depending on every fixed arity, label tuple and derivative order, and imposes no arity-growth control. The draft correctly leaves the growth hypotheses of a classical reconstruction theorem unverified.

Reconstruction and literature boundary

The final paragraph of reflection.tex correctly limits the proved result to Euclidean Hilbert-space dynamics. The hypotheses used here do not establish a selected pure phase, a unique invariant vacuum, local relativistic fields or a full analytic reconstruction. An internal Spec.Final already packages these data and therefore cannot be treated as the result of this semigroup lemma alone.

The original Osterwalder–Schrader paper is linked at https://projecteuclid.org/journals/communications-in-mathematical-physics/volume-42/issue-3/Axioms-for-Euclidean-Greens-functions-II-with-an-Appendix-by/cmp/1103899050.pdf. The parent reports inspecting section IV.1. This reviewer attempted to open that exact URL with the web tool; it returned an anti-bot iframe and the iframe fetch failed. Consequently this report does not independently certify the paper's detailed hypotheses or its applicability. The draft presently uses it only as an expressly outstanding possible route, which is consistent with this evidence boundary. Its bibliographic record was not part of the two reviewed files.

Extraction: changes required before mathematical acceptance

The initial extraction draft has substantive defects; it is not yet a verified conditional extraction theorem. These were sent directly to its author and the parent for correction.

  1. Wrong normalization. The draft describes the unsmeared lattice correlation U as including a_N^(4n). YM.Family.numeric, YM.Extraction.lifted, and extendCut are unsmeared moments without this quadrature factor. The factor belongs to Order.smears / the cell volume only. Leaving it inside U changes the continuum quantity under discussion.

  2. Generic is not noncoincident. YM.generic in Lean/YM/Space/Configurations.lean requires distinct projections in every coordinate, whereas conf only requires distinct points. YM.Extraction.Cyclic in Patch.lean excludes periodic equality in every coordinate of every pair. NoHit excludes only equality modulo the period of the full points. The draft equates generic with ordinary noncoincidence and later refers to extension through coincidences. Required extension is through coordinate ties while remaining off collisions; the source does not require smoothness through actual collisions.

  3. The size assumptions are too weak for the smearing conclusion. Source Bound.cyclic in Integrate.lean applies on compact subsets of NoHit, and Bound.volume on compact subsets of conf. The draft limits the bounds to Cyclic and generic sets. Compact bounds only away from coordinate-tie hyperplanes do not dominate a separated test that meets those hyperplanes: concentration in shrinking neighborhoods is not excluded. Nullity of the hyperplanes alone does not repair this. Restore the stronger source bounds, show the exceptional sets have measure zero, and supply the actual uniform dominators. In the source, intraperiod uses a constant times the indicator of the whole bounded fundamental box, not the original test support alone; ring_bound uses a compact thickening inside conf for the outer limit.

  4. Grid-only modulus does not directly permit evaluation at fixed dense centers. The source first defines rounded extensions extendCut on the full position domain and proves eventual oscillation on compact sets by thickening the compact set and using the uniform rounding error (delta, each_oscillates in Lipschitz.lean). Only then does localAscoli apply at fixed dense centers. The draft's Ascoli proof skips this step and speaks as if the grid-only functions were already equicontinuous functions on a common domain. Rounded extensions need not be individually continuous; the proof requires eventual oscillation, which is sufficient and should be stated accurately.

  5. Countability must concern basis signatures. Arbitrary tuples from nonzero finite-dimensional complex label spaces are uncountable. YM.Extraction.Indices in Stencil.lean indexes arity, grade tuple and basis-index tuple. The source diagonalizes over these countably many coordinates, then uses the finite multilinear expansion in components to recover every label tuple. The draft assumes countability of all label signatures and does not explain applicability to the actual family. State the basis reduction and prove the finite expansion passage.

  6. Suitability is only a volume-support condition. Separation from collisions comes from membership in the separated test space. Patch.suitable only places the support strictly inside the physical cell. Correct the attribution and retain both conditions in the domination proof.

  7. Separate smeared from point reflection positivity. The closed-cone finite-sum argument is correct if each matrix entry has the asserted common limit. But for a nonempty word w the diagonal reflection configuration (theta w)w necessarily has coordinate ties: reflection leaves spatial coordinates unchanged. Perturbing w and reflecting it preserves these ties. Generic point limits therefore cannot pass even these diagonal entries. The source's Order.realPositive in Harvest.lean proves smeared reflection positivity using ordered smear limits and exact finite reflection forms; assembles then obtains point positivity from smoothness and the smeared result, through YM.Table.Pairing.at_point in Euclid/RPPeak.lean. The draft must supply this route, or explicitly assume and justify a stronger point convergence statement covering these nongeneric configurations. Generic extraction alone is insufficient.

The source evidence file also needs declaration-namespace correction: lattice moment identities are YM.Family.oriented, YM.Family.anchored, and YM.Family.real_eval; Extraction/LatticeFamily.lean is their path, not a namespace. Its claim that source smoothness passes through coincident configurations must be replaced by the actual off-collision, through-coordinate-ties statement.

Finite Wilson law: no substantive defect found

The finite action and plaquette formula match YangMills.plaquette, wilsonEnergy, and wilsonLaw in PublicWilson.lean. The action uses each i<j face once. Nonnegativity follows from unitarity and the diagonal-entry bound. Compactness and continuity make the exponential bounded and integrable for every real beta; its strict positivity against a probability measure gives positive partition function. Normalization is then legitimate. Gauge transformation cancels intermediate vertex factors, leaves a conjugate plaquette, and preserves the product Haar measure by separate fixed left/right translations on each link. These arguments are mathematically correct.

Corresponding internal declarations directly read: YM.Wilson.energy_nonneg, energy_cont, partition_pos, the probability instance and continuous_integrable in Lean/YM/Wilson/Law.lean; prior_gauge, law_preserved, and law_gauge in Lean/YM/Wilson/Symmetry.lean. The draft explicitly distinguishes the finite statements from all outstanding RG and continuum estimates, and does not misuse undefined integrals as expectations. One editorial integration change is required: its P_p,D_N notation differs from the statement's L_p,d_N, and section label sec:ex-lattice differs from the parent reference ex-lattice.

Assembly: valid conditional implication

The OPE inequalities, phase-uniform remainder quantifier, and finite-channel sums agree with Hierarchy.OperatorProductExpansion. The trace coefficient and finite mode/track comparison class agree with Hierarchy.TraceTest. The absolute-convergence argument for each mode is sound: on a sphere, continuity bounds the homogeneous numerator; exponential decay controls infinity, and for mass zero the radial near-zero power is a+b+1, which is integrable. The value assigned to the integrand at the single massless origin is irrelevant to the integral. The draft correctly does not claim this integrability argument proves profile exclusion.

The full-mesh identification lemma correctly uses cofinality of an increasing integer subsequence and uniqueness of row limits. Eventual suitability is available from the earlier setting; retain that dependency. The final assembly preserves the family-before-volume order and one common hierarchy, but its joint input already supplies every substantive endpoint property. Its explicitly conditional wording is therefore essential. No additional mathematical defect was found in this implication at the recorded content.

Further source inspection used Patch.lean, Lipschitz.lean, Integrate.lean, Stencil.lean, Ties.lean, and Harvest.lean under Lean/YM/Extraction, plus the finite Wilson modules above. Initial review of the ground-algebra chapter

Reviewed parent manuscript/existence/phases.tex, SHA-256 a7f68251ec76a5864e0fa288ce91a7e1ecdaf5dc60c028df6cccb6e0ccd1d0fc. Direct source reads include YM.OS.Drive.against, YM.OS.QRead, size, producer in OS/Train.lean; the projection and spatial-invariance source already recorded; the full Ground/Compress.lean and Ground/Moments.lean; and the four-cluster argument in Ground/Permute.lean.

No substantive defect was found in the displayed conditional proofs. The train hypothesis bounds the squared norms by B_v D^(4n); the triangle inequality extends this to all finite sums with growth D^(2n). Symmetry of the formal T=I_sharp I and the supremum argument give the bounded insertion estimate without presupposing null-quotient compatibility. Null compatibility is a conclusion of that estimate. The source hypothesis QRead uses the absolute Gram diagonal, which equals precisely this squared norm. The source does not require a uniform B_v over all exterior words.

The placement conventions check: translating theta(w tau_d v) u by d gives theta(v) tau_d(theta w) tau_d(u); choosing positive placements of theta p and q with amounts r,s produces the time difference x_time-r-s. The whole-word compression is independent of width and placement. For positive v,w, the concatenated slab word tau_d(theta v) tau_d(w) is the translate of (theta v)w, so compression gives X(v)X(w), not a product with an extra intervening projection. The adjoint relation M(theta v)=M(v) follows, also for translated words.

The large-time spatial-invariance argument correctly uses full O(4) covariance, including the label action, and positive time components before and after the rotation. Its estimate remains valid for varying transverse displacement. The separated-cluster induction makes the same point explicitly: its varying transverse unitary fixes the projected limit, while contractivity controls the varying vector error. Adjacent time-gap divergence suffices for the pairwise time gaps used by the source, by finite summation. The four-cluster exchange uses the time/spatial swap with displacements in the required order; the preceding rotation identity applies with b=e_time+e_space fixed by that swap. Testing between projected word vectors then proves commutation on the whole ground space. Projecting the dense word span proves cyclicity; commutativity makes that cyclic vacuum separating. Empty words and the empty cluster list are included.

Two minor clarity edits were requested: state that x_0,e_0 denote the chosen time coordinate/axis despite the initial time-last convention, and insert an absolute value around the final complex inner product in the train Cauchy-Schwarz estimate. The existing equality identifies that inner product as real nonnegative, so this is a notation clarification rather than a false inequality.

The chapter explicitly assumes the repeated-word bound for each slab and full orthogonal covariance; it does not prove either from the Wilson trajectory. It also stops before norm separability, the representing measure and the phase-table properties, as it should. The new source map in phase-interface-review.md records those obligations. No build was run for this chapter by this reviewer.

Phase-selection review and literature check

The two minor ground-algebra edits are present in parent phases.tex, SHA-256 862384926a1451e5afccedc8a51f23ee9e2030d661347474a7288e28c5061c6f. Reviewed initial phase-selection.tex, SHA-256 0c420334bc0d0b240f864d7e81e23411bdcdadad6ad33c38e1a7dbacb5ff27f3.

The representing-measure proposition is valid. The algebra is nonzero because its vacuum has norm one. Characters on a C*-algebra are continuous, so evaluations on a norm-dense countable sequence separate them; compactness turns the embedding into a homeomorphism onto its image. The positive normalized vacuum functional gives a probability measure, and cyclicity/separation from the preceding chapter proves full support using a nonzero continuous function vanishing outside a proposed zero-measure open set.

Original literature was independently opened with the web tool: Williams, A (Very) Short Course on C*-Algebras, dated 14 February 2024, Theorem 2.13, printed p. 29, gives the required commutative Gelfand theorem; Sawyer, Lecture Notes in Advanced Real Analysis, Chapter 6, Theorem 32, printed p. 78, gives representation of a positive complex-linear functional on C_c(X) for locally compact Hausdorff X. Here compact metrizability ensures these topological hypotheses and C_c(X)=C(X). The draft's applications are appropriate. Williams Theorem 2.19, printed p. 34, supplies continuous functional calculus for a normal element; requested its explicit citation at the square-root step proving phase reflection positivity. Only theorem statements and the immediately relevant context were inspected; this is not an independent reproof of those background theorems.

Whole-word character values obey the barycenter identity. Phase reflection positivity follows because the finite Gram combination is XX as an operator on the ground space, belongs to the unital C-subalgebra, and has its positive square root in that subalgebra. This supplies an actual phase-positivity argument that was still an obligation in the earlier source map. The remaining phase regularity and covariance requirements are correctly left separate.

The common countable set of rational-position, finite-basis-labelled positive words works in every phase under multilinearity and off-collision continuity. Actual raw-label finite dimensionality was checked directly in Euclid/Register.lean and Labels/Reference.lean: Ref d is the complex function space on Frame.Index=Fin(extra+1). The initial draft's basis-expansion sentence needs the brief distinction between free formal words and their Hilbert vectors: pair the proposed vector difference with every word vector, use multilinearity of scalar correlations, and then density to prove the vector equality. This clarification was sent to the parent. Positional approximation follows from the norm-square Gram formula; all reflected configurations remain off collisions even with spatial ties. No phase-dependent choice of approximating basis or rational positions is required.

The almost-everywhere ground-space argument matches Phase/Unique.lean and is valid. Its f_n are nonnegative continuous functions of the character, dominated by f_0 on compact X. Dominated convergence identifies their integral limit using the unselected heat projection. The character integral of M(v)*M(v) gives the same squared projected norm, so the nonnegative variance has integral zero. Its measurability follows from the pointwise limit and the continuous character term, without needing a measurable field of Hilbert spaces. The one common countable word family permits the full-measure intersection; projecting its dense span yields the one-dimensional ground space. Probability ensures that this full-measure set is nonempty. This is existence of a selectable phase, not a claim that every character phase is already pure.

Initial one-step flat-packet review: revisions required

Read drafter flat-step.tex, SHA-256 6fcce198b27ac035999152ca92790087ae028a42357d68b0ca75cd7a39a982bd, and flat-step-evidence.md. Read actual YM.RG08.Flat.toward and poised in RG08/Packets/FlatStep.lean, the full relevant matching arguments in Packets/Pushed.lean and Torus.lean, and the definitions/estimates in Pert/Bulk/{Projected,Atlas,Discrete}.lean and Packets/Lines.lean. The following revisions were sent to the drafter and parent before integration.

  1. The finite-label sums are inconsistent. Source RowCost and ColCost each fix both finite labels and sum only the cover coordinate. The manuscript defines the column sum over the cover and the input label but later inserts another factor m for that input label. Its centering lemma states a fixed output label with factor m, yet the convolution proof uses that bound as if output labels were already summed. To retain the correct m²q(2/tau)c/L coefficient, use source fixed-label atlas conventions, prove the centered fixed-label row bound without m, and then sum over outgoing labels once to get m. The root label sum supplies the other m.

  2. The final proposition needs explicit tau>0, 0<=sigma<=omega, 2sigma<=Ltau and nonnegative costs/r. Saying only that the kernels meet the atlas assumptions does not state these independent width conditions.

  3. Matching and compatible periodization are not defined, and the one-paragraph regrouping claim cannot establish the asserted matrix product. Define tori Z^4/fZ^4 and Z^4/cZ^4 with f=Lc; matching means the output-residue sum at every cover root x equals the matrix row indexed by [x]_f. State city covariance T(x+Lu,y+u)=T(x,y). Transpose matching is the change of variables sum_k T(a+fk,y)=sum_k T(a,y-ck). Matching of a convolution follows by first periodizing the final index and then partitioning the intermediate coordinate into finite residue classes. Two such compositions give M^T A N. This is exactly the source mechanism, and is short enough to write fully.

  4. Distances must use the cover component of a cover-label pair. The initial E(x,v)=sep(x,v) is otherwise ill-typed. In coordinate-path telescoping, negative edges use the forward difference based at the other endpoint; both endpoints stay within the path length of the root. State this to connect the atlas's forward-difference estimate to a path in arbitrary directions.

  5. Absolute summability should be made explicit before using neutrality, travel or matching. The uncentered total absolute sum at fixed coarse root is bounded by (m q L²) r (m c L^-2)=m²qcr. This independently justifies regrouping; the centered estimate subsequently improves the quantitative cost by L^-1.

The weighted geometry and the final finite-matrix orientation are sound once these conventions are fixed. The scalar centering inequality follows from a exp(sigma a/L)<=a exp(tau a/2)<=(2/tau)exp(tau a). The root kernel is matched by the Y carrier and the outgoing kernel by the H carrier; inherited identities then place N_A on the left and M_A on the right, agreeing with the mixed precision identity. The latter is explicitly assumed in this draft; the source proves it from more primitive primal/dual identities. No independent claim that this manuscript has derived those primitives is warranted. Final acceptance of this local proof is pending the revised text.

Operator regularity and norm separability

Parent phases.tex now has SHA-256 caa09a94e0eb08b4d5d87728a4d1d283de508c9bf72d800bbef2080de9e38d64. Its new rotation clarification matches YM.OS.Ground.rotate and trans in Ground/Lean.lean: rotation acts on quotient field labels, which may be returned to raw coordinates by arbitrary representative choices; invariance of correlations makes those choices immaterial. An honest raw-label O(4) action is not assumed. The conditional ground-algebra proofs need only the stated covariance and its translation/concatenation compatibility.

Read parent slab-regularity.tex, SHA-256 30403cd8854c4546485ba675a3d5abcb2a07815323fdb01d35637f7573c789a2, against the full Lean/YM/Work/B02/Analysis.lean and Slab.lean. No substantive mathematical or source-correspondence defect was identified. The matrix-element remainder argument applies the real mean-value inequality to complex scalar observations and then uses density to obtain the operator norm bound. It does not presuppose operator-norm continuity of the operator family. The dense test space consists of finite word sums, and the derivative bounds scale with their actual Hilbert norms, as required; a mere list of individual word estimates would not suffice.

At a fixed slab configuration the finite positive time margins and sup-norm pair distances give a common neighborhood with positive buffer. AllCopy bounds each fixed derivative-labelled tuple locally, even when the chosen raw representative maps are nonlinear. The derivative candidate is real-linear in the coordinate increment because it is a finite sum against coordinate functionals. Scalar smoothness and the exact slot identity, including exterior words, give its matrix elements. The first remainder estimate proves norm continuity of every tuple map. Applying this to the finitely many derivative tuples proves continuity of the derivative candidate and hence the true operator-norm derivative. Induction simultaneously over all label tuples then gives every finite derivative order. This reproduces the source's columnD_bound, plant_deriv, cont_place, deriv_place, and smooth_place logic without assuming continuity at the stage where it must be proved.

The norm-separability corollary is valid: scalar multilinearity first gives bounded-operator multilinearity by testing on two dense word spans. Every generator can be placed in positive time, put in a rational-width slab, expanded in finite bases, and approximated in operator norm at rational positions. Compression is bounded and therefore retains this convergence. Rational-complex star polynomials in the resulting countable generator set are countable and dense. This supplies the precise norm-separability requirement of the character construction from the displayed regularity inputs; Hilbert-space separability alone would not have supplied it.

The chapter still assumes existence of bounded slab insertions, the uniform AllCopy estimate, smooth off-collision scalar correlations and exact raw-slot derivatives. It proves operator-norm smoothness and algebra norm separability from those inputs, not their lattice origin. No Lean or LaTeX build was performed by this reviewer.

Individual reflection and orthogonal covariance

The bound R_k need not be uniform in k. The error is uniform over copy pairs, and finitely many eventualities can be intersected at fixed m. Positive translation directions separate different copies in every coordinate. The original wording omitted internal genericity, which translations cannot create; the final text correctly restricts this stage to internally generic grid words and later uses norm continuity on all distinct configurations.

For real basis labels, reflected conjugation agrees with ordinary reflection up to word reversal, so D*=−D. This is a shorter valid variant of B06's three cases. The elementary dimension induction proves that these reflections generate O(4), respecting the quotient group action. Ordinary conjugation and transport of coordinate-axis positivity then follow with the stated raw signed compatibility.

The remaining substantive manuscript bridge is the individual itinerary expansion and rooted price calculation. Pedi.canon/plurality bounds finite matrix words, sums the colour indices, and integrates the glide parameter on a common neighborhood. With c colours and correction order n, the determinant word (A-1)P_s(-E_s)^(n+1) has n+3 factors, cap amplitude c^4(a+1)ht and ratio ct; the symmetric correction D(-sD)^n has n+1 factors, amplitude c^2 w and ratio cw. Their derivative scales are the respective word lengths times the common factor scale. The quadratic word (-D)^(n+1)A has n+2 factors; two endpoint colour sums and protected Gaussian coordinate bounds p give amplitude p^2 c^4 a w and ratio cw. Nonhot endpoint and locally zero cutoff cases must first give zero germs; unarmed paths vanish by the 3B finite-range property. The source endpoint bound is Moraine.occupancy; it cannot be replaced by a bound on arbitrary Gaussian coordinates.

Tax.scalar/Export.out provide an essential volume-uniform rooted sum, which is stronger than convergence in operator norm. At halo m>=4, let K=ballCost(3B+2)L^4|Axis|. Armed n+u paths from one initial bond number at most K^(n+u). A visited fixed root restricts that initial bond to radius (n+u+1)(mB+1), giving at most ballCost((u+1)(mB+1))16^nL^4*|Axis| choices. The support cover is at most (n+u+1)steady(m). Thus the weighted root sum is bounded by HC times the geometric series of ratio Q16Kexp(Asteady(m)+2 epsilon), where H=ballCost((u+1)(mB+1))L^4|Axis|K^uexp(A(u+1)steady(m)). Ratio at most 1/2 gives 2HC. StreamsCost.fluid first absorbs the derivative length factor by replacing C by C*[1+(1+u)d]^j and Q by Q*2^j. Negative upper exponent of Q beats the subpower geometric count, while H preserves C's exponent. This is the precise missing mathematical content between the existing chapters and StreamsCost.enlighten/Corbel.waits. Sent the formulas and source locations to parent; no manuscript edits.
